[3,5,2,1,4] ^s27tgzoI

- This alogrithm says if array range is from 1 t N or 0 to N then we can use cyclic sort.
- Means N = 5 then numbers should be from 1 to 5 
- In each case correct index for value is value - 1 when ( 1 to N ) and value when ( 0 to N ).

1. In worst case we swapped N times equal to length of Array + traversed each number in array
O(N - 1) for traversal and O(N) for swaps thus = O(N) time complexity at worst.
2. Space complexity is constant. ^ue6YvwFj

def cycliSort(nums: Array[int]) return sorted Array[int] {
    i = 0
    while i < nums.length {
        correctPosition = nums[i] - 1
        if correctPosition != i {
             swap(nums, i, correctPosition)
        }else {
            ++i
        }
    }
}

def swap(arr: Array[int], first: int, second: int){
    temp = arr[first]
    arr[first] = arr[second]
    arr[second] = temp
} ^36jNOgKg

Works fine for cases with no duplicates  ^HlshXWKq

The Problem Scenario
Consider: [1,1,3,4,5] (has duplicate)
Your code: if correctPosition != i

i=0: nums[0]=1, correctPosition=0, 0==0 → increment
i=1: nums[1]=1, correctPosition=0, 0!=1 → swap with itself infinitely!

My suggestion: if nums[correctIndex] != nums[i]

i=1: nums[1]=1, correctIndex=0, nums[0]=1, 1==1 → detects duplicate, increments ^dlmk3YlK

def cycliSort(nums: Array[int]) return sorted Array[int] {
    i = 0
    while i < nums.length {
        correctIndex = nums[i] - 1
        if nums[correctIndex] != nums[i] {
             swap(nums, i, correctPosition)
        }else {
            ++i
        }
    }
}

def swap(arr: Array[int], first: int, second: int){
    temp = arr[first]
    arr[first] = arr[second]
    arr[second] = temp
} ^StPmlufc

Takes care of duplicate as will increment if sees a duplicate instead of infinite swap ^csjfgKHM
